<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the fixed-degree bulk-universality conjecture for random regular graphs. For every fixed integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3 and every fixed energy in the open Kesten–McKay bulk, the unfolded eigenvalue point process of the adjacency matrix of a uniform simple labelled <i>d</i>-regular graph converges to the GOE bulk process. The convergence holds along all admissible graph sizes, without additional conditioning.

- **claim-main-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed integer $d\ge3$, every fixed $E\in(-2\sqrt{d-1},2\sqrt{d-1})$, and every nonnegative continuous compactly supported $h:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$, $$\left\| \mathbb E\exp\!\left(-\int h\,\mathrm d\Xi_{n,d,E}\right) - \mathbb E\exp\!\left(-\int h\,\mathrm d\Xi_n^{\mathrm{GOE}}\right) \right\|\longrightarrow0$$ as $n\to\infty$ through all admissible sizes.
  - Explanation: For fixed degree and fixed bulk energy, the Laplace functional of the unfolded graph eigenvalue process is asymptotically that of the GOE bulk process, along all admissible sizes.
- **claim-configuration-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-model)): In the configuration model with $d$ stubs at each of $n$ labelled vertices, conditioning the matching to give a simple graph produces the uniform simple labelled $d$-regular graph. Moreover, $$\mathbb P(\text{the matching gives a simple graph}) \longrightarrow \exp\!\left(-\frac{d^2-1}{4}\right)>0$$ along all admissible sizes. In the simple model, a specified vertex belongs to a triangle with probability $O(n^{-1})$. For any fixed $k$, independently sampled uniform oriented edges have distinct endpoints and induce exactly a matching with probability $1-O_k(n^{-1})$.
  - Explanation: Conditioning the configuration matching on simplicity yields the uniform simple regular graph, and simplicity has asymptotically positive probability; local defects are rare.
- **claim-spectral-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-spectral-input)): There are constants $c_0,c_1>0$, $B<\infty$, and a fixed open interval $I_E$ containing $E$, with closure contained in $(-2\sqrt{d-1},2\sqrt{d-1})$, such that the following hold with probability tending to one.* 1. *After omitting the eigenvalue associated with the constant vector, the ordered eigenvalues are within $n^{-c_0}$ of their Kesten–McKay classical locations. In the normalization above, every eigenvector entry is bounded by a deterministic $n^{o(1)}$.* 2. *Uniformly for $\Re\zeta\in I_E$ and $S/n\le\Im\zeta\le1$, $$\left\|\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(A-\zeta)^{-1}-m_d(\zeta)\right\| \le(\log n)^B\bigl(n^{-c_1}+(n\Im\zeta)^{-c_1}\bigr).$$ Except at at most $n^{1-c_1}$ centers, the resolvent entries on a center and its $d$ neighbors satisfy the same error bound relative to the corresponding infinite $d$-regular tree entries.* *These conclusions hold along all admissible $n$. In particular, $$\#\{i:\|x_i\|\le S^2\}=O(S^2)$$ on an event of probability tending to one.
  - Explanation: Imported rigidity, delocalization and local resolvent control hold with probability tending to one, together with a bound on the number of eigenvalues in a microscopic window.
- **claim-tree-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-identities)): Let $q(\zeta)$ be the solution of $$\zeta=q^{-1}+(d-1)q$$ which tends to zero at infinity in the upper half-plane. Then $$-m_d(\zeta)=\frac1{\zeta-dq(\zeta)}.$$ The regular-tree resolvent has diagonal entry $m_d(\zeta)$ and adjacent entry $(1+\zeta m_d(\zeta))/d$. At $\zeta=E+\mathrm i\eta$, its imaginary-part matrix on a star, divided by $\pi\rho$, tends at a polynomial rate in $\eta$ to the covariance matrix with variances one, center-neighbor covariance $p$, and distinct-neighbor covariance $$\frac{E^2-d}{d(d-1)}.$$ Furthermore, $$k_0=\frac{1+Em_*}{d} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad k_0^2-k_0-m_*^2=0.$$ For $U(E)=\int\log\|t-E\|\rho_d(t)\,\mathrm dt$, $$\label{eq:tree-potential} -U(E)=-\frac12\log d+\frac{d-1}{2}\log a -\frac d4\log(1-p^2).$$ Replacing $E$ by $E+\mathrm i\eta$ in the logarithmic potential changes its real part by $O(\eta)$.
  - Explanation: Gives the regular-tree resolvent entries, the star covariance of the Gaussian wave and the closed form of the logarithmic potential.
- **claim-edge-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-edge-moments)): Fix positive integers $k,\ell$ and a nonnegative integer $q$. There are constants $c,C>0$ and events of graphs of probability tending to one with the following property. Choose distinct indices $i(1),\ldots,i(\ell)$ satisfying $\|x_{i(j)}\|\le S^2$, by any rule depending only on the graph. Independently sample $k$ uniform oriented edges $(v_\alpha,v'_\alpha)$, conditionally on the graph, and set $$b_j=\bigl(u_{i(j)}(v_1),\ldots,u_{i(j)}(v_k), u_{i(j)}(v'_1),\ldots,u_{i(j)}(v'_k)\bigr)^\top .$$ For every coordinate monomial $P$ of total degree at most $q$, $$\left\|\mathbb E\bigl[P(b_1,\ldots,b_\ell)\mid A\bigr] -\mathbb EP(Z_1,\ldots,Z_\ell)\right\|\le Cn^{-c}, \qquad Z_1,\ldots,Z_\ell\ \text{independent }N(0,C_k).$$ The constants and graph events are uniform over the indicated choices of eigenindices. They may depend on $d,E,k,\ell,q$; no assertion is made for dimensions or moment orders growing with $n$.
  - Explanation: Fixed joint moments of eigenvector values at sampled edge endpoints are polynomially close to Gaussian moments, uniformly over graph-determined eigenindex choices.
- **claim-type-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-type-count)): Suppose $M=M_n$ and, for some fixed $\kappa>0$, $M^{d+1}\log n\le n^{1-\kappa}$. With probability tending to one, simultaneously for every pair of histograms $T,Q$, the number of vertex labelings with these histograms is at most $$\label{eq:ent-type-count} \exp\left\{nH(T)-\frac{nd}{2}H(Q)+O(n^{1-c})\right\},$$ where $c>0$ and the error constant depend only on $d,\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Counts vertex labelings with prescribed star and edge histograms by an entropy bound, uniformly over all histograms.
- **claim-star-edge-entropy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-star-edge)): On events of graphs of probability tending to one, uniformly over the choices of $V$ and $0\le\epsilon\le1$, $$\label{eq:ent-star-edge} \mathcal H(F_{\rm star})-\frac d2\mathcal H(F_{\rm edge}) \ge \frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global})-O(n^{-c}),$$ where $Y_{\rm global}$ is the complete $n\ell$-dimensional array. Moreover, $$\label{eq:ent-global-entropy} \frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global}) =\ell\left(\frac12\log\frac{2\pi{\rm e}\eta}{\pi\rho} -U(E)\right)+O(n^{-c}), \qquad U(E)=\int\log\|t-E\|\rho_d(t)\,\,\mathrm dt .$$
  - Explanation: Relates differential entropies of smoothed star and edge observations to the entropy of the global Gaussian array.
- **claim-projected-entropy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-projected-entropy)): On the graph events above, uniformly for $0\le\epsilon\le1$ and each $i$, $$\label{eq:ent-projected-entropy} \mathcal H(\epsilon B_V+G) -\frac d2\mathcal H(P_i(\epsilon B_V+G)) \ge -O(n^{-c}).$$ Moreover $B_V$ has mean zero and second-moment matrix $I_D+O(n^{-1+o(1)})$. Every fixed moment of $P_iB_V$ is invariant up to $O(n^{-1+o(1)})$ under the orthogonal map interchanging $w$ and $v_i$, acting trivially on the colors.
  - Explanation: After removing the near-deterministic eigenvector direction, an entropy inequality holds together with second-moment identities and an approximate swap symmetry.
- **claim-tensor-coercivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-tensor)): For every fixed $j\ge3$, there exists $c_j>0$ such that every symmetric $j$-tensor $H$ on $D$ satisfying $\sigma_i^{\otimes j}P_i^{\otimes j}H=P_i^{\otimes j}H$ for all $i$ obeys $$\label{eq:ent-tensor} \\|H\\|^2-\frac12\sum_{i=1}^d\\|P_i^{\otimes j}H\\|^2 \ge c_j\\|H\\|^2 .$$
  - Explanation: A strict quadratic coercivity holds for symmetric tensors invariant under the local swaps, which is where the open bulk and degree at least three enter.
- **claim-gaussian-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-gaussian-criterion)): Fix an integer $q\ge2$. Let $B_n$ be random vectors in $D$ with $\\|B_n\\|\le K_n$ almost surely, where $K_n=n^{o(1)}$ is deterministic. Suppose that for some $c_*>0$, $$\\|\mathbb EB_n\\|+\\|\mathbb EB_nB_n^{\mathsf T}-I_D\\|=O(n^{-c_*}),$$ and, for every $1\le j\le q$ and every $i$, $$\left\\|(\sigma_i^{\otimes j}-I) \mathbb E(P_iB_n)^{\otimes j}\right\\|=O(n^{-c_*}).$$ Let $G$ be an independent standard Gaussian in $D$. Suppose also that, uniformly for $0\le\epsilon\le1$, $$\mathcal H(\epsilon B_n+G) -\frac12\sum_{i=1}^d\mathcal H(P_i(\epsilon B_n+G)) \ge-O(n^{-c_*}).$$ Then every fixed polynomial moment of $B_n$ of degree at most $q$ differs from its standard Gaussian value by $O(n^{-c})$, for some $c>0$. The conclusion is uniform over families satisfying these hypotheses with the same $c_*$, error constants, and bound $K_n$.
  - Explanation: Entropy deficit bounds plus tensor coercivity force fixed-order moments of the random vectors to approach Gaussian moments polynomially.
- **claim-endpoint-control** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint-control)): There is a deterministic $\xi>0$, depending only on $d,E$, with the following property. For every fixed $k\ge1$ and $D>0$, there are constants $C_{k,D}<\infty$ and $y_{k,D}<\infty$ such that, for every fixed $s\in\mathbb R$ and every fixed $y\ge y_{k,D}$, $$\label{eq:lim-endpoint-control} \limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\left( \|m_n(s+\mathrm i y)-m_*\| +\\|M_{n,k}(s+\mathrm i y)-K_k(m_*)\\|>y^{-\xi} \right) \le C_{k,D}y^{-D}.$$ The limit is through admissible graph sizes. The constants and the height threshold are independent of $s$; the argument $s+\mathrm i y$ is fixed before taking the limit.
  - Explanation: At large fixed microscopic heights, the scalar and endpoint-matrix resolvents concentrate near their tree values with polynomially small failure probability.
- **claim-microscopic-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-microscopic-limit)): From every sequence of admissible sizes tending to infinity one can extract a subsequence along which $$\bigl(\nu_n,m_n,(M_{n,k})_{k\ge1}\bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \bigl(\nu,m,(M_k)_{k\ge1}\bigr)$$ jointly, in the local vague topology for the point measures and the compact-open topology on the upper half-plane for the analytic functions. The limiting point measure $\nu=\sum_j\delta_{x_j}$ is locally finite, with multiplicities permitted. There are deterministic $\xi,\chi>0$, depending only on $d,E$, such that the following properties hold.* *For every fixed $0<\delta<1$, almost surely for all sufficiently large $R$, $$\label{eq:mesoscopic-limit} m(e+\mathrm i u)=m_*+O(u^{-\xi}), \qquad \|e\|\le R,\quad R^\delta\le u\le R^2,$$ and, almost surely, $$\label{eq:discrepancy} \nu([v,w])=\rho(w-v)+O(R^{1-\chi}), \qquad -R\le v\le w\le R,\quad R\longrightarrow\infty.$$ The constants and starting values of $R$ in these almost sure statements may depend on the sample and on $\delta$, but the exponents are deterministic.* *On a possible extension of the probability space, for every fixed $k$ there are standard Gaussian vectors $g_j\in\mathbb R^{2k}$, independent over $j$ conditionally on $\nu$, such that $$\label{eq:marked-representation} \begin{split} m(z)&=m_{\rm r}+\mathop{\mathrm{PV}}\sum_j\frac1{x_j-z},\\ M_k(z)&=K_k(m_{\rm r}) +\mathop{\mathrm{PV}}\sum_j \frac{C_k^{1/2}g_jg_j^{\mathsf T}C_k^{1/2}}{x_j-z}. \end{split}$$ These representations are consistent as $k$ increases by retaining the first $k$ edge channels. A separate Gaussian mark is assigned to each occurrence of a repeated point. The principal values converge locally uniformly on the upper half-plane, and $\Im M_k(z)$ is positive definite there, almost surely simultaneously for every finite $k$ and every $z$ with $\Im z>0$.
  - Explanation: Subsequential joint limits of the point process and resolvents exist, with mesoscopic resolvent control, power-saving counts and a principal-value Gaussian-mark representation.
- **claim-continuous-switching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-continuous-switching)): For every fixed $k\ge2$ and every $O\in O(k)$, the joint analytic-function law of $(m,M_k)$ is invariant under $\mathcal T_O$.
  - Explanation: The limiting law of the scalar and marked resolvents is invariant under the orthogonal switching action.
- **claim-gaussian-insertion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion)): The coefficients in <a href="#eq:sw-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sw-moments">[eq:sw-moments]</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:sw-coefficient-bounds} \\|\beta\\|\le C\theta,\qquad cT\le s_2\le CT,\qquad \|s_1\|\le CT,$$ with deterministic positive constants. Moreover, as $r\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:sw-trace-limit} m_{\rm r}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(H_r-z)^{-1} \ \Longrightarrow\ m(z)$$ jointly at every finite list of points $z\in\mathbb C_+$, and the unscaled eigenvalue point measure of $H_r$ converges locally vaguely in law to $\nu$. Both convergences are unconditional.* *More precisely, generate the Gaussian marks of $M_k$ conditionally on $\nu$, separately for each $r$, and write $$\mathcal S_r(z) =m(z)-\partial_z\log\det(I_{2k}+BM_k(z)).$$ For almost every $\nu$, expectations of every bounded smooth function with bounded derivatives of finitely many real and imaginary trace values differ by $o(1)$ when $m_{\rm r}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(H_r-z)^{-1}$ is replaced by $\mathcal S_r(z)$. This is a conditional approximation assertion, not conditional switching invariance.
  - Explanation: The coefficients are controlled and the limiting point process is realized as a limit of a diagonal matrix with scalar shift and GOE noise.
- **claim-weighted-blocks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-weighted-blocks)): For $\mathcal A=I,F$, put $a_I=h$, $a_F=K$. With probability tending to one, $$\label{eq:sw-bernstein} \left\\|U_{\mathcal A} (A'_{\mathcal A\mathcal A}-s_1I) U_{\mathcal A}\right\\| \le \operatorname{polylog}(r) \left(\sqrt{T/a_{\mathcal A}}+\theta\right).$$ Moreover, for $P_0=U_IA'_{IF}U_F$, $$\label{eq:sw-cross-block} \\|P_0\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le T\operatorname{polylog}(r)$$ with probability tending to one.
  - Explanation: Matrix Bernstein bounds control the weighted diagonal blocks and the Hilbert–Schmidt size of the weighted cross block.
- **claim-far-block-schur** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-schur)): Let $$\delta_F=\operatorname{polylog}(r) \left(\frac{1+T}{K}+\sqrt{\frac TK}+\theta\right).$$ Then $$\label{eq:sw-far-resolvent} \\|U_F^{-1}(G_F-D_F^{-1})U_F^{-1}\\|\le\delta_F=o(1)$$ with probability tending to one, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}G_F=o_{\mathbb P}(1)$. For $\Sigma=A'_{IF}G_FA'_{FI}$, $$\label{eq:sw-schur-small} \\|U_I\Sigma U_I\\|=o_{\mathbb P}(T^{-5}).$$
  - Explanation: The far block is close to its diagonal part, its trace is negligible, and the weighted Schur correction is small.
- **claim-trace-cut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-trace-cut)): For every fixed $z\in\mathbb C_+$, $$\label{eq:sw-inner-resolvent} \\|U_I^{-1}G_I(z)U_I^{-1}\\|=O_{\mathbb P}(h),$$ and $$\label{eq:sw-second-cut} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(D+A'-z)^{-1} =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(D_I+A'_{II}-z)^{-1}+o_{\mathbb P}(1).$$ The same estimates hold jointly for a fixed finite list of $z$’s.
  - Explanation: The inner resolvent is controlled at larger height and the full trace equals the inner-block trace up to a vanishing error.
- **claim-gaussian-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-lindeberg)): Conditional on almost every $\nu$, bounded smooth tests with bounded derivatives of finitely many trace values have expectation difference $o(1)$ between $$D_I+A'_{II} \quad\hbox{and}\quad H_r=D_I+s_1I_{q_*}+\sqrt{s_2}\,\mathcal W.$$
  - Explanation: A summand-by-summand replacement shows smooth trace statistics of the perturbed block match those of the scalar-shifted GOE model.
- **claim-dbm-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-dbm-input)): Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m$ satisfies <a href="#eq:dbm-regularity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-regularity">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href="#eq:dbm-parameter-domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-parameter-domain">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\label{eq:dbm-time-window} g m^\sigma\le t\le m^{-\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\pi$. For every fixed positive integer $a$ and $O\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^a)$, the difference $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:dbm-marginal-test} &\int_{\mathbb R^a}O(\boldsymbol\alpha) p_{V+\sqrt t\,W_m}^{(a)} \left(\frac{\pi\alpha_1}{m},\ldots, \frac{\pi\alpha_a}{m}\right) \,\mathrm d\boldsymbol\alpha \notag\\ &\hspace{12mm}- \int_{\mathbb R^a}O(\boldsymbol\alpha) p_{W_m}^{(a)} \left(\frac{\pi\alpha_1}{m},\ldots, \frac{\pi\alpha_a}{m}\right) \,\mathrm d\boldsymbol\alpha \longrightarrow0. \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the expected sums of $O$ over ordered tuples of distinct points of the two eigenvalue processes, both rescaled by $m/\pi$, differ by $o(1)$.
  - Explanation: For regular deterministic diagonal data in the time window with matched density at zero, marginal local statistics agree with those of GOE.
- **claim-regularity-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dbm-regularity-density)): For almost every $\nu$, the matrices $V_*$ are eventually regular with $$g=\frac{T^{1/2}}{q_*},\qquad G=q_*^{-1/8}.$$ They satisfy the hypotheses <a href="#eq:dbm-parameter-domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-parameter-domain">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a> and <a href="#eq:dbm-time-window" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-time-window">[eq:dbm-time-window]</a> with $m=q_*$ and $\delta=\sigma=\tau$. Moreover, $$\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t_*}(0)\longrightarrow\frac1\pi.$$ If $c_r=\pi\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t_*}(0)$, then eventually $c_r>0$, and the data $c_rV_*$ and time $c_r^2t_*$ satisfy Theorem <a href="#thm:dbm-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:dbm-input">20</a>, including its exact density condition.
  - Explanation: The rescaled diagonal matrices are regular with the stated parameters and the free-convolution density at zero tends to the semicircle value, after an exact dilation.
- **claim-goe-counts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-goe-counts)): For every compact interval $J\subset\mathbb R$ and every $b>0$, $$\sup_{m\ge1}\mathbb E\exp\bigl(b\,\mathcal G_m(J)\bigr)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Exponential moments of GOE counts in a compact interval are bounded uniformly in dimension.

At $\\zeta=E+\\mathrm i\\eta$, its imaginary-part matrix on a star, divided by $\\pi\\rho$, tends at a polynomial rate in $\\eta$ to the covariance matrix with variances one, center-neighbor covariance $p$, and distinct-neighbor covariance $$\\frac{E^2-d}{d(d-1)}.$$ Furthermore, $$k_0=\\frac{1+Em_*}{d}\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n k_0^2-k_0-m_*^2=0.$$ For $U(E)=\\int\\log|t-E|\\rho_d(t)\\,\\mathrm dt$, $$\\label{eq:tree-potential}\n -U(E)=-\\frac12\\log d+\\frac{d-1}{2}\\log a\n                       -\\frac d4\\log(1-p^2).$$ Replacing $E$ by $E+\\mathrm i\\eta$ in the logarithmic potential changes its real part by $O(\\eta)$.",
      "explanation": "Gives the regular-tree resolvent entries, the star covariance of the Gaussian wave and the closed form of the logarithmic potential.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tree-identities",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q(\\zeta)$ be the solution of",

        "anchor": "stmt-ent-star-edge",
        "exact_quote": "On events of graphs of probability tending to one, uniformly over the choices of $V$ and $0\\le\\epsilon\\le1$, $$\\label{eq:ent-star-edge}\n \\mathcal H(F_{\\rm star})-\\frac d2\\mathcal H(F_{\\rm edge})\n \\ge \\frac1n\\mathcal H(Y_{\\rm global})-O(n^{-c}),$$ where $Y_{\\rm global}$ is the complete $n\\ell$-dimensional array.         "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $q\\ge2$. Let $B_n$ be random vectors in $D$ with $\\|B_n\\|\\le K_n$ almost surely, where $K_n=n^{o(1)}$ is deterministic. Suppose also that, uniformly for $0\\le\\epsilon\\le1$, $$\\mathcal H(\\epsilon B_n+G)\n -\\frac12\\sum_{i=1}^d\\mathcal H(P_i(\\epsilon B_n+G))\n \\ge-O(n^{-c_*}).$$ Then every fixed polynomial moment of $B_n$ of degree at most $q$ differs from its standard Gaussian value by $O(n^{-c})$, for some $c>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-microscopic-limit",
        "exact_quote": "From every sequence of admissible sizes tending to infinity one can extract a subsequence along which $$\\bigl(\\nu_n,m_n,(M_{n,k})_{k\\ge1}\\bigr)\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n \\bigl(\\nu,m,(M_k)_{k\\ge1}\\bigr)$$ jointly, in the local vague topology for the point measures and the compact-open topology on the upper half-plane for the analytic functions. The limiting point measure $\\nu=\\sum_j\\delta_{x_j}$ is locally finite, with multiplicities permitted. There are deterministic $\\xi,\\chi>0$, depending only on $d,E$, such that the following properties hold.*\n\n\n\n*For every fixed $0<\\delta<1$, almost surely for all sufficiently large $R$, $$\\label{eq:mesoscopic-limit}\n m(e+\\mathrm i u)=m_*+O(u^{-\\xi}),\n \\qquad |e|\\le R,\\quad R^\\delta\\le u\\le R^2,$$ and, almost surely, $$\\label{eq:discrepancy}\n \\nu([v,w])=\\rho(w-v)+O(R^{1-\\chi}),\n \\qquad -R\\le v\\le w\\le R,\\quad R\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ The constants and starting values of $R$ in these almost sure statements may depend on the sample and on $\\delta$, but the exponents are deterministic.*\n\n\n*On a possible extension of the probability space, for every fixed $k$ there are standard Gaussian vectors $g_j\\in\\mathbb R^{2k}$, independent over $j$ conditionally on $\\nu$, such that $$\\label{eq:marked-representation}\n \\begin{split}\n  m(z)&=m_{\\rm r}+\\mathop{\\mathrm{PV}}\\sum_j\\frac1{x_j-z},\\\\\n  M_k(z)&=K_k(m_{\\rm r})\n       +\\mathop{\\mathrm{PV}}\\sum_j\n       \\frac{C_k^{1/2}g_jg_j^{\\mathsf T}C_k^{1/2}}{x_j-z}.\n \\end{split}$$ These representations are consistent as $k$ increases by retaining the first $k$ edge channels. A separate Gaussian mark is assigned to each occurrence of a repeated point. The principal values converge locally uniformly on the upper half-plane, and $\\Im M_k(z)$ is positive definite there, almost surely simultaneously for every finite $k$ and every $z$ with $\\Im z>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The limiting point measure $\\nu=\\sum_j\\delta_{x_j}$ is locally finite, with multiplicities permitted.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "The coefficients in <a href=\"#eq:sw-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sw-moments\">[eq:sw-moments]</a> satisfy $$\\label{eq:sw-coefficient-bounds}\n \\|\\beta\\|\\le C\\theta,\\qquad\n cT\\le s_2\\le CT,\\qquad |s_1|\\le CT,$$ with deterministic positive constants. Moreover, as $r\\to\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:sw-trace-limit}\n m_{\\rm r}+\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(H_r-z)^{-1}\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\ m(z)$$ jointly at every finite list of points $z\\in\\mathbb C_+$, and the unscaled eigenvalue point measure of $H_r$ converges locally vaguely in law to $\\nu$. Both convergences are unconditional.*\n\n*More precisely, generate the Gaussian marks of $M_k$ conditionally on $\\nu$, separately for each $r$, and write $$\\mathcal S_r(z)\n =m(z)-\\partial_z\\log\\det(I_{2k}+BM_k(z)).$$ For almost every $\\nu$, expectations of every bounded smooth function with bounded derivatives of finitely many real and imaginary trace values differ by $o(1)$ when $m_{\\rm r}+\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(H_r-z)^{-1}$ is replaced by $\\mathcal S_r(z)$. This is a conditional approximation assertion, not conditional switching invariance.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.2"

This is a conditional approximation assertion, not conditional switching invariance.",

        "anchor": "stmt-sw-weighted-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\mathcal A=I,F$, put $a_I=h$, $a_F=K$. With probability tending to one, $$\\label{eq:sw-bernstein}\n \\left\\|U_{\\mathcal A}\n       (A'_{\\mathcal A\\mathcal A}-s_1I)\n       U_{\\mathcal A}\\right\\|\n \\le \\operatorname{polylog}(r)\n       \\left(\\sqrt{T/a_{\\mathcal A}}+\\theta\\right).$$ Moreover, for $P_0=U_IA'_{IF}U_F$, $$\\label{eq:sw-cross-block}\n \\|P_0\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\le T\\operatorname{polylog}(r)$$ with probability tending to one.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dbm-input",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:dbm-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-regularity\">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:dbm-parameter-domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-parameter-domain\">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:dbm-time-window}\n g m^\\sigma\\le t\\le m^{-\\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\\rho_{\\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\\pi$. For every fixed positive integer $a$ and $O\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^a)$, the difference $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{eq:dbm-marginal-test}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{V+\\sqrt t\\,W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,\\mathrm d\\boldsymbol\\alpha \\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}-\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,\\mathrm d\\boldsymbol\\alpha\n \\longrightarrow0.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the expected sums of $O$ over ordered tuples of distinct points of the two eigenvalue processes, both rescaled by $m/\\pi$, differ by $o(1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-dbm-input",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **admissible size** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Here admissible means that this set is nonempty.
- **uniform random regular graph** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For an admissible integer $n$, let $G_{n,d}$ be uniform among the simple labelled $d$-regular graphs on $\{1,\ldots,n\}$.
- **spectral scale S** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Put $$S=\exp(\sqrt{\log n}).$$
- **channel** ([source](#pm-h-5)): We call each sampled edge and its two endpoint coordinates a channel.
- **principal value** ([source](#pm-h-23)): For a locally finite point measure $\nu=\sum_j\delta_{x_j}$, a principal value below always means the symmetric position truncation $\lim_{R\to\infty}\sum_{\|x_j\|\le R}$.
- **(g,G)-regular diagonal matrix** ([source](#pm-h-38)): For positive parameters $g,G$, we call $V$ $(g,G)$-regular if, for fixed positive constants $c,C,C_V$,

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-simplicity-limit | limiting probability that the matching is simple | = | $\exp\!\left(-\frac{d^2-1}{4}\right)$ | fixed d, n tending to infinity |
| q-spectral-scale | subpolynomial spectral scale | = | $\exp(\sqrt{\log n})$ | n tending to infinity |
| q-rigidity-error | eigenvalue rigidity error | ≤ | $n^{-c_0}$ | fixed d and E, probability tending to one |
| q-edge-moment-error | Gaussian moment approximation error at sampled edges | ≤ | $Cn^{-c}$ | constants depend on d,E,k,l,q |
| q-endpoint-failure | endpoint concentration failure probability | ≤ | $C_{k,D}y^{-D}$ | fixed k, D, s and large fixed y |
| q-count-discrepancy | limiting point count discrepancy | = | $\rho(w-v)+O(R^{1-\chi})$ | R tending to infinity |
| q-inner-cutoff | inner position cutoff | = | $r^{1/100}$ | r a positive integer tending to infinity |
| q-dbm-lower-scale | regularity lower height | = | $\frac{T^{1/2}}{q_*}$ | almost every limiting law, large r |
| q-kernel-diagonal | projection kernel diagonal | ≤ | $2\sqrt{2m}$ | uniform in m and location |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-model): Count stub realizations and use factorial moments of defects with inclusion-exclusion. -> Uniform simple graph with positive asymptotic simplicity probability.
- **step-spectral** [claim](#stmt-prop-spectral-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spectral-input): Import fixed-degree rigidity and local law results and rescale. -> Rigidity, delocalization and local resolvent control.
- **step-tree** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-identities): Schur complement on the tree and boundary imaginary parts. -> Tree resolvent, covariance and logarithmic potential.
- **step-type-count** [claim](#stmt-ent-type-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-type-count): Configuration-model first moment with Stirling bounds. -> Uniform entropy bound on labelings.
- **step-star-edge** [claim](#stmt-ent-star-edge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-star-edge): Quantize, apply the type count, and compute the global Gaussian entropy. -> Star-edge entropy inequality.
- **step-projected** [claim](#stmt-ent-projected-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-projected-entropy): Remove the eigenvector residual direction and match constants with the potential. -> Projected entropy inequality and swap symmetry.
- **step-tensor** [claim](#stmt-ent-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-tensor): Weighted frame identity and commutator kernel argument, then compactness. -> Tensor coercivity.
- **step-criterion** [claim](#stmt-ent-gaussian-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-gaussian-criterion): Induction on Hermite degree using the entropy expansion and coercivity. -> Gaussian moment matching.
- **step-edge-moments** [claim](#stmt-prop-edge-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-gaussian-criterion-2): Apply the criterion to the projected star vector and factor over independent edges. -> Uniform endpoint moments.
- **step-endpoint** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-control): Descend in height using concentration and a two-edge switch scalar equation. -> Endpoint resolvent control.
- **step-microscopic** [claim](#stmt-prop-microscopic-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-microscopic-limit): Tightness, extraction of limits, Gaussian marks and identification of constants. -> Marked microscopic limit.
- **step-switching** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuous-switching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuous-switching): Permutation switches, orthogonal conjugation and generation of the orthogonal group. -> Orthogonal switching invariance.
- **step-blocks** [claim](#stmt-sw-weighted-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-weighted-blocks): Matrix Bernstein inequality after truncation. -> Weighted block bounds.
- **step-schur** [claim](#stmt-sw-schur) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-schur): Neumann expansion and second-moment bounds. -> Far block and Schur correction bounds.
- **step-trace-cut** [claim](#stmt-sw-trace-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-trace-cut): Iterated resolvent identity and Schur complement. -> Trace restricted to the inner block.
- **step-lindeberg** [claim](#stmt-sw-lindeberg) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-lindeberg): One-summand replacement with Taylor expansion. -> Gaussian replacement.
- **step-insertion** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-lindeberg-2): Combine the cut estimates, switching invariance and trace-to-point-measure inversion. -> Limit law as finite matrix with GOE noise.
- **step-dbm-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-dbm-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-dbm-input): Specialize the cited fixed-energy theorem and rescale multiplicities. -> Marginal GOE comparison for regular diagonal data.
- **step-regularity** [claim](#stmt-dbm-regularity-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dbm-regularity-density): Verify regularity from the limit estimates and locate the free-convolution root. -> Hypotheses of the Dyson Brownian motion input.
- **step-goe-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-goe-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-goe-counts): Gaussian decimation and projection-kernel bounds. -> Uniform exponential count moments.
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-goe-counts-2): Bracket Laplace functionals by inclusion-exclusion sums, then pass from subsequences to the full sequence. -> Fixed-energy bulk universality.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-bulk-laplace-universality**: For fixed degree and fixed bulk energy, the Laplace functional of the unfolded eigenvalue point process of the uniform simple regular graph approaches that of the GOE reference process as the size grows through admissible sizes.
- **inv-no-extra-conditioning**: The conclusion is for the unconditioned uniform simple regular graph; no connectedness or similar property is imposed.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-size-certificate**: Use the theorem to certify a quantitative error margin for the spectral statistics of one finite graph. -> The proof obtains the limit by extracting subsequences, so it yields convergence only, with no explicit rate for any given order.
- **anti-growing-degree**: Apply the bulk statement to graphs whose degree grows with the order. -> All constants and the limiting objects depend on a fixed degree, so nothing is claimed when the degree varies.
- **anti-edge-energies**: Read the theorem as describing eigenvalues at or near the spectral edge. -> The energy is restricted to the open interval, so edge behaviour is not covered.
- **anti-growing-moment-orders**: Use the endpoint moment estimates with dimensions or moment orders that grow with the graph size. -> The estimates are proved only for fixed dimensions and orders.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic convergence statements proved by tightness and subsequence extraction; there is no finite procedure with explicit inputs, outputs and finite-size rates to implement or test. The source says one can extract a subsequence along which the limit holds, which is existential.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem is a convergence statement along subsequences of graph sizes and gives no procedure or finite-size error rate, so it cannot be turned into a library routine. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model is the plain uniform simple regular graph with no extra graph property imposed, so it supplies no mechanism for generating or steering simulated worlds. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only exact sampling fact used is that conditioning a pairing model on simplicity gives the uniform simple regular graph. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-degree** (hypothesis): The theorem holds only for a fixed integer degree at least three; all constants and limiting objects depend on that degree, and nothing is claimed when the degree varies with the size. Blocked use: ['Applying the bulk universality statement to graphs whose degree grows with the number of vertices']
- **limit-open-bulk-energy** (hypothesis): The energy is fixed inside the open Kesten–McKay bulk interval, so spectral edge eigenvalues and energies at the boundary are not covered. Blocked use: ['Using the result for extremal eigenvalues or spectral gap claims', 'Using the result at energies on or outside the boundary of the bulk']
- **limit-nonconstructive-subsequence** (nonconstructive): The limit is obtained by extracting subsequences and identifying their limits, so only convergence is established; no explicit finite-size rate and no algorithm are provided. Blocked use: ['Certifying a quantitative error margin for the spectra of a single finite graph', 'Treating the proof as a computational routine with a running time']
- **limit-fixed-moment-orders** (hypothesis): The endpoint moment estimates are for a fixed number of sampled edges, fixed number of eigenvectors and fixed moment order; nothing is asserted when these grow with the size. Blocked use: ['Applying the Gaussian moment estimates with growing dimensions or moment orders']
- **limit-unweighted-simple-graph** (formal_scope): The model is the uniform simple labelled regular graph with unweighted adjacency matrix; weighted, directed or irregular graphs are outside the statement. Blocked use: ['Transferring the GOE bulk law to weighted, directed or non-regular networks']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the fixed-degree bulk-universality conjecture for random regular graphs. For every fixed integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3 and every fixed energy in the open Kesten–McKay bulk, the unfolded eigenvalue point process of the adjacency matrix of a uniform simple labelled <i>d</i>-regular graph converges to the GOE bulk process. The convergence holds along all admissible graph sizes, without additional conditioning.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A random regular graph is sparse even when its order tends to infinity: each row of its adjacency matrix has exactly $d$ nonzero entries. Nevertheless, its bulk eigenvalues are expected to exhibit the local statistics of a real symmetric Gaussian matrix. We prove this prediction for every fixed degree $d\ge3$, at each fixed energy in the interior of the limiting spectrum.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Main result

For an admissible integer $n$, let $G_{n,d}$ be uniform among the simple labelled $d$-regular graphs on $\{1,\ldots,n\}$. Here admissible means that this set is nonempty. Let $A_{n,d}$ be its unweighted adjacency matrix, with eigenvalues $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_n$, counted with multiplicity. No conditioning on connectedness, bipartiteness, or another graph property is imposed.

The limiting density is the Kesten–McKay density $$\rho_d(t)=\frac{d\sqrt{4(d-1)-t^2}}{2\pi(d^2-t^2)}
 \qquad \bigl(|t|<2\sqrt{d-1}\bigr).$$ For a fixed energy $E$ in this interval, define the unfolded point measure $$\Xi_{n,d,E}=\sum_{i=1}^n
       \delta_{\,n\rho_d(E)(\lambda_i-E)}.$$ For comparison, let $H_n$ be a normalized GOE matrix: its entries on and above the diagonal are independent centered Gaussians, with variance $1/n$ off the diagonal and $2/n$ on the diagonal. If $\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_n$ are its eigenvalues, put $$\Xi_n^{\mathrm{GOE}}
       =\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{(n/\pi)\mu_i}.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every fixed integer $d\ge3$, every fixed $E\in(-2\sqrt{d-1},2\sqrt{d-1})$, and every nonnegative continuous compactly supported $h:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$, $$\left|
 \mathbb E\exp\!\left(-\int h\,\mathrm d\Xi_{n,d,E}\right)
 -
 \mathbb E\exp\!\left(-\int h\,\mathrm d\Xi_n^{\mathrm{GOE}}\right)
 \right|\longrightarrow0$$ as $n\to\infty$ through all admissible sizes.*

</div>

The GOE reference point measures converge in law for the vague topology (Valkó and Virág 2009) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[ValkoVirag](#ref-ValkoVirag)]. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> identifies the full local point-process limit, not only its one-point density or an energy-averaged statistic. It proves the fixed-degree bulk-universality conjecture stated in (Bourgade and Huang 2026, Conjecture 2.11) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BourgadeHuang](#ref-BourgadeHuang)], in a fixed-energy Laplace-functional formulation.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Background and significance

The density above originates in the spectral measure of the regular tree and the limiting spectrum of random regular graphs (Kesten 1959; McKay 1981) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Kesten](#ref-Kesten), [McKay](#ref-McKay)]. It describes eigenvalue counts on macroscopic intervals, but does not determine the spacings between consecutive eigenvalues. Jakobson, Miller, Rivin, and Rudnick (Jakobson et al. 1999, sec. 1 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[JakobsonEtAl](#ref-JakobsonEtAl)] formulated the GOE prediction for fixed-degree regular graphs and gave numerical evidence for it. Their empirical nearest-neighbor spacing formulation precedes the fixed-energy point-process formulation studied here. The prediction is particularly striking at fixed degree: local tree geometry persists, whereas the proposed eigenvalue statistics agree with those of a dense Gaussian matrix.

The development of local laws separated spectral control from the identification of microscopic statistics. Bauerschmidt, Knowles, and Yau (Bauerschmidt, Knowles, and Yau 2017) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BauerschmidtKnowlesYauLocal](#ref-BauerschmidtKnowlesYauLocal)] <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BauerschmidtEtAl](#ref-BauerschmidtEtAl)] established a local semicircle law for growing degrees using local resampling. Bauerschmidt, Huang, Knowles, and Yau (Bauerschmidt et al. 2017, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) proved bulk gap universality and energy-averaged correlation universality in a polynomial degree range. More recently, Bourgade and Huang (Bourgade and Huang 2026, Proposition 2.10) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BourgadeHuang](#ref-BourgadeHuang)] obtained fixed-energy point-process universality for $(\log n)^{24}\ll d\le n^{1/2}$ by loop equations, and stated the remaining fixed-degree problem in their Conjecture 2.11.

For fixed degree the local reference law is Kesten–McKay. Bauerschmidt, Huang, and Yau (Bauerschmidt, Huang, and Yau 2019) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BauerschmidtHuangYauLocal](#ref-BauerschmidtHuangYauLocal)] <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HuangYau](#ref-HuangYau)] established the local law and delocalization for sufficiently large fixed degrees. Huang and Yau (Huang and Yau 2024) subsequently proved rigidity, complete delocalization, and local resolvent estimates for every fixed $d\ge3$. Their estimates supply the spectral input for this paper. They control smoothed eigenvalue counts and typical tree neighborhoods, while the conclusion sought here concerns unsmoothed statistics at one fixed energy on the scale of an individual spacing.

For every fixed $d\ge3$, Huang, McKenzie, and Yau (Huang, McKenzie, and Yau 2025, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HuangMcKenzieYau](#ref-HuangMcKenzieYau)] proved optimal eigenvalue rigidity, up to subpolynomial factors, and GOE edge universality. Their edge result identifies the joint fluctuations of any fixed number of extreme nontrivial eigenvalues at either spectral edge.

Eigenvector Gaussianity provides the link we need from spectral control to microscopic bulk statistics. The Gaussian-wave model on a regular tree appears in (Elon 2008; Csóka et al. 2015) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ElonEigenvectors](#ref-ElonEigenvectors), [CsokaEtAl](#ref-CsokaEtAl)]. Backhausz and Szegedy (Backhausz and Szegedy 2019, Theorems 2.1 and 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BackhauszSzegedy](#ref-BackhauszSzegedy)] proved qualitative Gaussian-wave limits for almost eigenvectors of random regular graphs, allowing a variance factor between zero and one in the weak limit. Their argument uses entropy inequalities for local observations; the underlying colored-star counting framework was developed in (Backhausz and Szegedy 2018) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BackhauszSzegedyTrees](#ref-BackhauszSzegedyTrees)] and further studied in (Backhausz, Bordenave, and Szegedy 2022) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BackhauszBordenaveSzegedy](#ref-BackhauszBordenaveSzegedy)]. Recent developments include variance-one Gaussian waves for spectral-edge eigenvectors (He, Huang, and Yau 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HeHuangYauWaves](#ref-HeHuangYauWaves)] and an extension of the entropy framework to graphs of finite cone type (Dembo and McKenzie 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DemboMcKenzie](#ref-DemboMcKenzie)]. Our argument develops this entropy method into a different input: polynomially accurate, variance-one joint moments at sampled graph edges. The estimates hold conditional on each graph in an event of probability tending to one, uniformly over finitely many chosen bulk eigenvectors. Weak convergence alone does not provide these high-moment estimates. The proof below supplies them through a strict tensor estimate and a quantitative Hermite expansion.

Reversible edge switches are also established tools in local laws and universality; see (Bauerschmidt et al. 2017, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BauerschmidtEtAl](#ref-BauerschmidtEtAl)] and (Bauerschmidt, Huang, and Yau 2019, sec. 7.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BauerschmidtHuangYauLocal](#ref-BauerschmidtHuangYauLocal)]. We use Gaussian endpoint marks to extend the discrete symmetry to an orthogonal action on an exact microscopic limiting law. Increasing the number of sampled edge pairs within that limit then inserts a Gaussian matrix perturbation. The last step uses the local relaxation of Dyson’s matrix evolution (Dyson 1962; Landon and Yau 2017) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DysonBrownian](#ref-DysonBrownian), [LandonYau](#ref-LandonYau)], in the fixed-energy form proved by Landon, Sosoe, and Yau (Landon, Sosoe, and Yau 2019) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[LandonSosoeYau](#ref-LandonSosoeYau)]. To obtain the full point-process conclusion from fixed-order correlations, we also give a compact-count bound using the Gaussian superposition and decimation identity of Forrester and Rains (Forrester and Rains 2001) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[ForresterRains](#ref-ForresterRains)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

We first work in the coordinates $x_i=n(\lambda_i-E)$, before the last density factor $\rho_d(E)$ is applied. For a real eigenvector normalized to have squared norm $n$, sampling its values at the two ends of a uniform oriented edge produces a pair of random real numbers. We call each sampled edge and its two endpoint coordinates a channel. The first part of the proof shows that, for any fixed number of eigenvectors near $E$, every fixed joint moment of these sampled values is polynomially close to the corresponding Gaussian moment, with probability tending to one over the graph. The comparison consists of centered, unit-variance Gaussian pairs, independent across the eigenvectors, with covariance $E/d$ within each pair.

The following steps then identify every subsequential microscopic point-process limit.

1.  *Quantitative Gaussian marks.* Section <a href="#sec:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:entropy">3</a> proves the endpoint moment statement. A colored-star counting inequality becomes an entropy inequality after the eigenvectors are smoothed by a Gaussian field. The tree logarithmic potential evaluates the entropy cost. A strict tensor inequality turns the resulting entropy deficit into Gaussian moment matching by induction over Hermite degree.

2.  *Microscopic limits with controlled tails.* Section <a href="#sec:limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limits">4</a> uses a two-edge switch and the moment statement to control resolvents at large fixed microscopic heights. It then extracts the point process together with its Gaussian endpoint marks. The limiting resolvent has a symmetric principal-value representation, and the point counts have a power-saving discrepancy from constant density.

3.  *Continuous switching and Gaussian insertion.* Section <a href="#sec:switching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:switching">5</a> combines the discrete switch symmetry with orthogonal invariance of the Gaussian marks. This produces exact continuous switching identities for the microscopic limiting law. After two controlled truncations, many weak switches give finite diagonal matrices with additive GOE noise whose eigenvalue point processes converge to that same limiting law.

4.  *Identification at one energy.* Section <a href="#sec:dbm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dbm">6</a> verifies the hypotheses of fixed-energy Dyson Brownian motion universality for these diagonal matrices. An exact adjustment of the free-convolution density matches the normalizations of the two ensembles. A uniform exponential bound for compact GOE counts and finite inclusion-exclusion then upgrade correlation comparisons to the Laplace functionals in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Two parts of this argument are useful beyond the final identification. The entropy calculation gives Gaussian moment approximation with polynomially small errors on common graph events of probability tending to one, including graph-dependent choices of eigenvectors; its strictness is a finite-dimensional tensor fact tied to the open bulk and to $d\ge3$. The continuous switching argument converts a discrete finite-rank symmetry into Gaussian perturbations of an exact limiting marked process. The latter mechanism requires only fixed-dimensional Gaussian information before the limit.

The passage from finitely many Gaussian marks to many weak switches takes place after the graph-size limit. We first let $n\to\infty$ with fixed numbers of sampled edges and fixed spectral windows, then control the tails to construct the principal-value marked law in Section <a href="#sec:limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limits">4</a>. Its switching identities are exact for every fixed number of channels. Only within this law do we let the channel number $k=2r$ tend to infinity, with the two spectral cutoffs prescribed in Section <a href="#sec:switching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:switching">5</a>. Finally we increase the order of the inclusion-exclusion brackets in Section <a href="#sec:dbm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dbm">6</a>. Thus the finite-graph argument requires Gaussian approximation only in fixed dimensions and at fixed moment orders.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Spectral input and tree identities

Throughout the proof, $d\ge3$ and $|E|<2\sqrt{d-1}$ are fixed. All constants may depend on these two parameters. This section records the spectral information used below and the tree identities that determine the covariance of the eigenvector entries. The spectral estimates are established results; the subsequent entropy and switching arguments are proved in this paper.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Conventions and microscopic coordinates

Let $$\rho_d(t)=
 \frac{d\sqrt{4(d-1)-t^2}}{2\pi(d^2-t^2)}
 \mathbf 1_{\{|t|<2\sqrt{d-1}\}},
 \qquad
 m_d(\zeta)=\int_{\mathbb R}\frac{\rho_d(t)}{t-\zeta}\,\mathrm dt
 \quad(\Im\zeta>0).$$ Set $$\rho=\rho_d(E),\qquad p=\frac Ed,\qquad
 a=\frac{d}{d-1}(1-p^2),\qquad
 m_*=m_d(E+\mathrm i0)=m_{\rm r}+\mathrm i\pi\rho.$$ In particular, $\rho>0$, $a>0$, and $|p|<1$. For the adjacency eigenvalues and a graph-determined real orthogonal eigenbasis, use the normalization $$Au_i=\lambda_i u_i,\qquad
 \langle u_i,u_j\rangle=n\delta_{ij}.$$ An arbitrary measurable choice inside each eigenspace suffices. Define <a id="eq:micro-coordinates"></a>$$\label{eq:micro-coordinates}
 x_i=n(\lambda_i-E),\qquad
 \nu_n=\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{x_i},\qquad
 m_n(z)=\sum_{i=1}^n\frac1{x_i-z}
       =\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}R_n(z),
 \quad
 R_n(z)=(A-E-z/n)^{-1}.$$ The remaining density factor for unfolding is $\rho$; the final point coordinates are obtained by multiplying the $x_i$ by $\rho$. We write $\mathbb C_+=\{z\in\mathbb C:\Im z>0\}$ for the upper half-plane.

For $k\ge1$, let <a id="eq:channel-matrices"></a>$$\label{eq:channel-matrices}
 J_k=\begin{pmatrix}0&I_k\\ I_k&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad C_k=I_{2k}+pJ_k,\qquad
 K_k(m)=mC_k+d^{-1}J_k.$$ The order of the $2k$ coordinates will always be the starting endpoints of $k$ oriented edges, followed by their ending endpoints. The matrix $C_k$ is positive definite.

Unsubscripted finite-dimensional matrix norms are operator norms; $\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{HS}}$ denotes the Hilbert–Schmidt norm. The imaginary part of a matrix is its Hermitian imaginary part. For the complex symmetric matrices occurring here it also agrees with entrywise imaginary part. All tensor norms are induced by the stated Euclidean inner products. The notation $n^{o(1)}$, when used on high-probability events, denotes a deterministic subpolynomial upper bound. We write $O_{\mathbb P}(b_r)$ for a random quantity whose ratio to $b_r$ is bounded in probability; $o_{\mathbb P}(b_r)$ means that the ratio tends to zero in probability.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The simple regular-graph model

We use the classical pairing model for regular graphs (Bollobás 1980) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BollobasPairing](#ref-BollobasPairing)]. The special counting facts needed here have the following direct proof.

<a id="stmt-lem-model"></a>
<div id="lem:model" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *In the configuration model with $d$ stubs at each of $n$ labelled vertices, conditioning the matching to give a simple graph produces the uniform simple labelled $d$-regular graph. Moreover, $$\mathbb P(\text{the matching gives a simple graph})
       \longrightarrow \exp\!\left(-\frac{d^2-1}{4}\right)>0$$ along all admissible sizes. In the simple model, a specified vertex belongs to a triangle with probability $O(n^{-1})$. For any fixed $k$, independently sampled uniform oriented edges have distinct endpoints and induce exactly a matching with probability $1-O_k(n^{-1})$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-model</summary>

*Proof.* Every simple graph has the same number $(d!)^n$ of stub realizations, which proves the conditional uniformity. A prescribed loop is one pair of stubs at a vertex. A prescribed double edge is an unordered pair of pairs between two distinct vertices, using distinct stubs. Let $Z$ count all occurring prescribed defects of these two kinds. The expected counts tend to $$\frac{d-1}{2}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\frac{(d-1)^2}{4},$$ respectively. Indeed, a prescribed family of $e$ disjoint pairs occurs with probability $$\frac{1}{(nd-1)(nd-3)\cdots(nd-2e+1)}
       \sim(nd)^{-e}$$ for fixed $e$.

For each fixed $j$, ordered $j$-tuples of defects on disjoint vertex sets give the leading contribution to $\mathbb E(Z)_j$. Compatible overlapping tuples contribute $o(1)$. To see this, regard their prescribed pairs as a multigraph with $v$ vertices and $e$ edges. Every vertex has degree at least two. A connected component all of whose degrees equal two can only be a single prescribed loop or a single prescribed double edge. Thus a genuinely overlapping component has $v<e$. There are $O(n^v)$ choices and each has probability $O(n^{-e})$. There are only finitely many overlap patterns for fixed $d,j$. Consequently $$\mathbb E(Z)_j\longrightarrow
 \left(\frac{d-1}{2}+\frac{(d-1)^2}{4}\right)^j.$$ The finite inclusion-exclusion bounds for $\mathbb P(Z=0)$, followed by increasing their order, give the asserted limit. Absence of these defects is exactly simplicity.

For a triangle through a fixed vertex, summing over the other two vertices and their stub choices gives $O(n^2)\,O(n^{-3})=O(n^{-1})$ in the matching model. Conditioning on simplicity changes this bound by a bounded factor. Finally, in any simple $d$-regular graph a uniform endpoint is a uniform vertex. Each previously chosen endpoint has only $O_d(1)$ vertices equal or adjacent to it. A union bound over the fixed number of sampled endpoints proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The imported spectral estimates

Put $$S=\exp(\sqrt{\log n}).$$ The following deliberately weak formulation is sufficient for our argument.

<a id="stmt-prop-spectral-input"></a>
<div id="prop:spectral-input" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Spectral input). *There are constants $c_0,c_1>0$, $B<\infty$, and a fixed open interval $I_E$ containing $E$, with closure contained in $(-2\sqrt{d-1},2\sqrt{d-1})$, such that the following hold with probability tending to one.*

1.  *After omitting the eigenvalue associated with the constant vector, the ordered eigenvalues are within $n^{-c_0}$ of their Kesten–McKay classical locations. In the normalization above, every eigenvector entry is bounded by a deterministic $n^{o(1)}$.*

2.  *Uniformly for $\Re\zeta\in I_E$ and $S/n\le\Im\zeta\le1$, $$\left|\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(A-\zeta)^{-1}-m_d(\zeta)\right|
     \le(\log n)^B\bigl(n^{-c_1}+(n\Im\zeta)^{-c_1}\bigr).$$ Except at at most $n^{1-c_1}$ centers, the resolvent entries on a center and its $d$ neighbors satisfy the same error bound relative to the corresponding infinite $d$-regular tree entries.*

*These conclusions hold along all admissible $n$. In particular, $$\#\{i:|x_i|\le S^2\}=O(S^2)$$ on an event of probability tending to one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-spectral-input"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-spectral-input</summary>

*Proof.* We use the fixed-degree results of Huang and Yau (Huang and Yau 2024, Theorems 1.2, 1.4, and 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[HuangYau](#ref-HuangYau)], in the precise version arXiv:2102.00963v3. Their matrix is $A/\sqrt{d-1}$; hence their bulk coordinate is $E/\sqrt{d-1}\in(-2,2)$. Rescaling the spectral parameter and the resolvent by this fixed factor gives our normalization.

For clarity, the relevant error and geometry can be checked directly in their Definition 4.1 and Equations (4.6)–(4.7). Choose fixed parameters $$0<c<1,\qquad a_{\mathrm{HY}}\ge12,\qquad
 b_{\mathrm{HY}}\ge25a_{\mathrm{HY}},\qquad
 0<\mathfrak r<c/32,$$ and neighborhood radius $r_{\mathrm{HY}}=\mathfrak r\log_{d-1}n$, rounded to an integer. Their Definition 1.1 and Proposition 2.1 give a tree-like event with probability tending to one, on which all but a polynomially small fraction of vertices have a tree neighborhood of radius $(c/4)\log_{d-1}n$. This is more than enough to contain every neighborhood needed for the resolvent approximation between two vertices of the same star. The tree extension with the semicircle weight in their Equation (2.12) is then the ordinary regular-tree Green function.

In a fixed bulk interval, their error is bounded by a logarithmic factor times $$(d-1)^{-r_{\mathrm{HY}}}
 +(n\Im\zeta)^{-1/2}
 +(n\Im\zeta)^{-2/3}.$$ The factor $S$ dominates every fixed power of $\log n$. After decreasing $c_1$ and increasing $B$, this gives the stated bounds and exceptional-set size. The same source provides polynomial rigidity and polylogarithmic complete delocalization. Its model and the quoted statements have no even-size restriction.

For the final assertion, apply the trace estimate at $\zeta=E+\mathrm iS^2/n$. Each eigenvalue with $|\lambda_i-E|\le S^2/n$ contributes at least $n/(2S^2)$ to $\Im(\lambda_i-\zeta)^{-1}$. The normalized imaginary trace is bounded, so the count is $O(S^2)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 4*. The estimates in Proposition <a href="#prop:spectral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spectral-input">3</a> are statements about events in the unconditioned simple-graph ensemble. We never condition the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> on connectedness, nonbipartiteness, or a tree-neighborhood property. The bounded Laplace observables are unaffected in the limit by discarding events of vanishing probability.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Covariance and logarithmic potential on the tree

The covariance below is the Gaussian-wave covariance on the regular tree; see (Elon 2008; Csóka et al. 2015) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[ElonEigenvectors](#ref-ElonEigenvectors), [CsokaEtAl](#ref-CsokaEtAl)]. We derive it together with the logarithmic potential, so that the entropy normalization is explicit.

<a id="stmt-lem-tree-identities"></a>
<div id="lem:tree-identities" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:tree-potential"></a>
**Lemma 5**. *Let $q(\zeta)$ be the solution of $$\zeta=q^{-1}+(d-1)q$$ which tends to zero at infinity in the upper half-plane. Then $$-m_d(\zeta)=\frac1{\zeta-dq(\zeta)}.$$ The regular-tree resolvent has diagonal entry $m_d(\zeta)$ and adjacent entry $(1+\zeta m_d(\zeta))/d$. At $\zeta=E+\mathrm i\eta$, its imaginary-part matrix on a star, divided by $\pi\rho$, tends at a polynomial rate in $\eta$ to the covariance matrix with variances one, center-neighbor covariance $p$, and distinct-neighbor covariance $$\frac{E^2-d}{d(d-1)}.$$ Furthermore, $$k_0=\frac{1+Em_*}{d}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 k_0^2-k_0-m_*^2=0.$$ For $U(E)=\int\log|t-E|\rho_d(t)\,\mathrm dt$, $$\label{eq:tree-potential}
 -U(E)=-\frac12\log d+\frac{d-1}{2}\log a
                       -\frac d4\log(1-p^2).$$ Replacing $E$ by $E+\mathrm i\eta$ in the logarithmic potential changes its real part by $O(\eta)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-tree-identities"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-tree-identities</summary>

*Proof.* A Schur complement at the root of a forward tree gives $q=(\zeta-(d-1)q)^{-1}$ in the convention $(\zeta-A)^{-1}$. Attaching $d$ forward branches yields the displayed formula for $-m_d$. The boundary value $\pi^{-1}\Im m_d(t+\mathrm i0)$ is the Kesten–McKay density above; the branch has the required mass at infinity and no additional pole.

The resolvent equation at the center and symmetry among its neighbors give the adjacent entry. Applying the same equation to a neighbor column gives, for the common distinct-neighbor entry $g_2$, $$m_d(\zeta)+(d-1)g_2
       =\zeta\,\frac{1+\zeta m_d(\zeta)}d.$$ Taking boundary imaginary parts proves the covariance formulas. The tree formula is analytic across each side of a sufficiently small bulk neighborhood, so these limits have a polynomial rate. Substituting its boundary value into $k_0=(1+Em_*)/d$ gives the quadratic identity.

To compute the potential, integrate its analytic derivative $-m_d(\zeta)=q/(1-q^2)$ after changing from $\zeta$ to $q$. Matching at infinity gives $$\Re\int\log(\zeta-t)\rho_d(t)\,\mathrm dt
       =-\log|q|-\frac{d-2}{2}\log|1-q^2|.$$ At the bulk boundary, $$|q|=(d-1)^{-1/2},\qquad
 |1-q^2|=\frac{\sqrt{d^2-E^2}}{d-1}.$$ These identities give <a href="#eq:tree-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-potential">[eq:tree-potential]</a> after substituting $a=d(1-p^2)/(d-1)$. The boundary logarithmic integral exists because $\rho_d$ is bounded near $E$. Finally, $$\int \left(\log|t-E-\mathrm i\eta|-\log|t-E|\right)\rho_d(t)\,\mathrm dt
 =\frac12\int\log\!\left(1+\frac{\eta^2}{(t-E)^2}\right)\rho_d(t)\,\mathrm dt
 =O(\eta),$$ by boundedness of the density near $E$, the substitution $t-E=\eta u$, and the integrable tail of $\log(1+u^{-2})$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:entropy"></a>
### Gaussian moments at sampled edges

We use the spectral estimates of Proposition <a href="#prop:spectral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spectral-input">3</a> to identify the empirical distribution of finitely many eigenvector coordinates at an edge. The estimate must be quantitative: a fixed moment will later be summed over a subpolynomial number of eigenindices. We develop the entropy framework of Backhausz and Szegedy (2019) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[BackhauszSzegedy](#ref-BackhauszSzegedy)] quantitatively to obtain the conditional moment accuracy required here.

<a id="stmt-prop-edge-moments"></a>
<div id="prop:edge-moments" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (Uniform endpoint moments). *Fix positive integers $k,\ell$ and a nonnegative integer $q$. There are constants $c,C>0$ and events of graphs of probability tending to one with the following property. Choose distinct indices $i(1),\ldots,i(\ell)$ satisfying $|x_{i(j)}|\le S^2$, by any rule depending only on the graph. Independently sample $k$ uniform oriented edges $(v_\alpha,v'_\alpha)$, conditionally on the graph, and set $$b_j=\bigl(u_{i(j)}(v_1),\ldots,u_{i(j)}(v_k),
           u_{i(j)}(v'_1),\ldots,u_{i(j)}(v'_k)\bigr)^\top .$$ For every coordinate monomial $P$ of total degree at most $q$, $$\left|\mathbb E\bigl[P(b_1,\ldots,b_\ell)\mid A\bigr]
       -\mathbb EP(Z_1,\ldots,Z_\ell)\right|\le Cn^{-c},
 \qquad Z_1,\ldots,Z_\ell\ \text{independent }N(0,C_k).$$ The constants and graph events are uniform over the indicated choices of eigenindices. They may depend on $d,E,k,\ell,q$; no assertion is made for dimensions or moment orders growing with $n$.*

</div>

We first prove the assertion for one sampled edge. Write $$V(v)=\bigl(u_{i(1)}(v),\ldots,u_{i(\ell)}(v)\bigr)\in\mathbb R^\ell.$$ A star observation consists of the value at a uniform vertex followed by its $d$ neighbor values in uniform random order. An edge observation uses a uniform oriented edge. Throughout this section the graph is conditioned on, and randomness in an observation includes its location. Write $H$ for discrete entropy and $\mathcal H$ for differential entropy, both with natural logarithms. Positive polynomial error exponents may decrease from one occurrence to the next.

The proof passes from colored-label counts to an entropy inequality for Gaussian-smoothed stars and edges. We remove the nearly deterministic eigenvector relation, then use tensor coercivity to turn the resulting entropy bound into Gaussian moment estimates.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Counting colored stars

We begin with a counting statement independent of eigenvectors. For a labeling of the vertices by an alphabet of size $M$, let $T$ be the probability distribution of the ordered star observation, symmetrized over all permutations of the neighbor positions. Let $Q$ be the oriented edge distribution. The counting argument follows the colored-star configuration-model framework of Backhausz and Szegedy (2018, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[BackhauszSzegedyTrees](#ref-BackhauszSzegedyTrees)]; see also the asymptotic expected-coloring formula of Backhausz, Bordenave, and Szegedy (2022, Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[BackhauszBordenaveSzegedy](#ref-BackhauszBordenaveSzegedy)]. We retain explicit errors for an alphabet growing with $n$.

<a id="stmt-ent-type-count"></a>
<div id="ent:type-count" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:ent-type-count"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Uniform type count). *Suppose $M=M_n$ and, for some fixed $\kappa>0$, $M^{d+1}\log n\le n^{1-\kappa}$. With probability tending to one, simultaneously for every pair of histograms $T,Q$, the number of vertex labelings with these histograms is at most $$\label{eq:ent-type-count}
 \exp\left\{nH(T)-\frac{nd}{2}H(Q)+O(n^{1-c})\right\},$$ where $c>0$ and the error constant depend only on $d,\kappa$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ent-type-count"><summary>Proof of stmt-ent-type-count</summary>

*Proof.* Work first in the configuration model, with an ordering of the $d$ half-edges at each vertex. Prescribe the vertex labels and the ordered list of desired neighbor labels at every vertex. For a fixed ordered tuple type $T_0$, there are at most $\exp(nH(T_0))$ such arrays, by the multinomial bound.

Let $m_{jk}=ndQ_{jk}$ be the number of half-edges whose vertex has color $j$ and whose prescribed neighbor has color $k$. Compatibility requires $m_{jk}=m_{kj}$, and $m_{jj}$ must be even. The probability that a uniform matching respects all these prescriptions is $$\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{j<k}m_{jk}!
                    \prod_j(m_{jj}-1)!!}{(nd-1)!!},$$ with zero-size factors omitted. Stirling’s bounds give its logarithm as $$-\frac{nd}{2}H(Q)+O(M^2\log n).$$ Thus the expected number of labelings of ordered type $T_0$ has the claimed exponential bound with $H(T_0)$ in place of $H(T)$.

Symmetrization increases entropy, so $H(T_0)\le H(T)$; it leaves $Q$ unchanged. There are at most $\exp(O(M^{d+1}\log n))$ possible ordered tuple types. Summing over those with a given symmetrization therefore changes only the sublinear error. Markov’s inequality, followed by a union bound over all types, gives <a href="#eq:ent-type-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-type-count">[eq:ent-type-count]</a> with a slightly larger sublinear allowance. The simplicity probability is bounded away from zero by Lemma <a href="#lem:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:model">2</a>, so the statement transfers to uniform simple graphs. The symmetrized histograms no longer depend on the half-edge ordering. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### A quantitative differential-entropy inequality

Choose small fixed parameters $\gamma,\alpha>0$ satisfying <a id="eq:ent-parameters"></a>$$\label{eq:ent-parameters}
 2\ell(d+1)\gamma<1,\qquad
 4\ell\gamma+\alpha<\frac14,\qquad
 0<\alpha<\gamma,$$ and take $\alpha$ smaller than the rigidity exponent in Proposition <a href="#prop:spectral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spectral-input">3</a>. Put $\eta=n^{-\alpha}$. Given the graph, let $Y$ have $\ell$ independent Gaussian colors, each with covariance <a id="eq:ent-noise"></a>$$\label{eq:ent-noise}
 \Gamma=\frac{1}{\pi\rho}\,
          \frac{\eta}{(A-E)^2+\eta^2}.$$ For $0\le\epsilon\le1$, set $F=\epsilon V+Y$. The parameter $\eta$ regularizes the eigenvector constraint; the independent parameter $\epsilon$ controls the size of the eigenvector signal. We retain the entropy inequality for every $\epsilon$, then choose a small power of $n^{-1}$ when extracting each fixed moment. All eigenvectors used here satisfy the uniform subpolynomial coordinate bound from Proposition <a href="#prop:spectral-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spectral-input">3</a>.

<a id="stmt-ent-star-edge"></a>
<div id="ent:star-edge" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:ent-star-edge"></a>
<a id="eq:ent-global-entropy"></a>
**Lemma 8** (Star–edge entropy). *On events of graphs of probability tending to one, uniformly over the choices of $V$ and $0\le\epsilon\le1$, $$\label{eq:ent-star-edge}
 \mathcal H(F_{\rm star})-\frac d2\mathcal H(F_{\rm edge})
 \ge \frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global})-O(n^{-c}),$$ where $Y_{\rm global}$ is the complete $n\ell$-dimensional array. Moreover, $$\label{eq:ent-global-entropy}
 \frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global})
 =\ell\left(\frac12\log\frac{2\pi{\rm e}\eta}{\pi\rho}
                    -U(E)\right)+O(n^{-c}),
 \qquad
 U(E)=\int\log|t-E|\rho_d(t)\,\,\mathrm dt .$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ent-star-edge"><summary>Proof of stmt-ent-star-edge</summary>

<a id="eq:ent-quantized"></a>
*Proof.* Quantize each coordinate into intervals of width $b=n^{-\gamma}$, clipping all values outside a range of order $[-n^\gamma,n^\gamma]$ into its two end bins. The resulting vertex alphabet has $M=n^{2\ell\gamma+o(1)}$ elements, so Lemma <a href="#ent:type-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ent:type-count">7</a> applies. Let $\mathcal L$ be the random quantized labeling of $F$, and let $T,Q$ be its random histograms. Entropy of the type index, followed by entropy bounded by logarithmic type size, gives $$\label{eq:ent-quantized}
 \frac1nH(\mathcal L)
 \le H(\mathbb ET)-\frac d2\mathbb EH(Q)+O(n^{-c}).$$ Here concavity was used only for the positive star term.

We next replace $\mathbb EH(Q)$ by $H(\mathbb EQ)$. Two independent edge locations have Gaussian observations whose off-diagonal covariance block has average Frobenius norm at most $$C\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\Gamma^2}{n^2}\right)^{1/2}
 \le \frac{C}{\sqrt n\,\eta}.$$ Indeed endpoints from different sampled edges are independent uniform vertices, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\Gamma^2\le Cn/\eta^2$. Each individual edge covariance has smallest eigenvalue at least $c_1\eta$, since $\|A\|\le d$. After whitening the two marginal blocks, Gaussian total variation from the product law is bounded by the off-block norm when that norm is small, and by one otherwise. The small-norm bound follows, for example, by differentiating the Gaussian density along the straight line joining the two covariance matrices. Averaging yields $$\mathbb E_{\rm locations}\,
 \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{\rm edge},Y_{\rm edge}'),
                 \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{\rm edge})\otimes
                 \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{\rm edge}')\bigr)
 \le \frac{C}{\sqrt n\,\eta^2},$$ where the marginal laws on the right are at the fixed sampled locations. Deterministic shifts and subsequent quantization do not increase this distance.

<a id="eq:ent-edge-concentration"></a>
Consequently each cell frequency of $Q$ has variance $O(n^{-1/2}\eta^{-2})$. There are $M^2$ edge cells, so $$\mathbb E\|Q-\mathbb EQ\|_1
 \le n^{4\ell\gamma-1/4+\alpha+o(1)}=O(n^{-c}).$$ The finite-alphabet entropy continuity bound $H_{\rm bin}(h)+h\log(M^2)$ at total variation distance $h$ now proves $$\label{eq:ent-edge-concentration}
 \mathbb EH(Q)=H(\mathbb EQ)+O(n^{-c}).$$

We record the differential-entropy passage carefully. Every fixed location covariance, including the complete array covariance, has spectrum in $[c_1\eta,C/\eta]$; every coordinate of the deterministic shift is $n^{o(1)}$. Unbounded binning and the entropy bound on a cube give $$\frac1nH(\mathcal L)
 \ge \frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global})
           -\ell\log b-O(n^{-c}).$$ The displayed error accounts for clipping. It is in fact superpolynomially small: one-coordinate Gaussian tails beyond $n^\gamma$, together with their quantized entropy contributions, are superpolynomially small uniformly in the shifts, because $\alpha<\gamma$. The loss for the full array is at most the sum of the coordinate losses. Translation by the deterministic array $\epsilon V$ does not change its differential entropy.

<a id="eq:ent-binning"></a>
For either fixed-dimensional local observation $X=F_{\rm star}$ or $F_{\rm edge}$, we also have $$\label{eq:ent-binning}
 H(\text{binned }X)
 =\mathcal H(X)-(\dim X)\log b+O(n^{-c}).$$ To justify the two-sided estimate, fill each bin uniformly. Bin averaging a Gaussian density changes it in total variation by at most $Cb\eta^{-1/2}$, by integrating its absolute first derivatives; the same bound holds for a mixture. Clipping adds only a superpolynomial tail error. Both resulting densities have polynomial supremum and second-moment bounds. Such bounds convert polynomial total variation into polynomial entropy error: subtract the common minimum of the two densities. The remaining densities have equal mass $h$; after division by $h$, their supremum and second moment are bounded by a polynomial divided by $h$, so their entropies have absolute value $O(1+\log(n/h))$. The entropy errors in mixing them with the common part are at most the binary entropy. This proves <a href="#eq:ent-binning" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-binning">[eq:ent-binning]</a>, and will also apply to fixed-dimensional projections below.

The laws $\mathbb ET,\mathbb EQ$ are exactly the binned local laws. Substitute <a href="#eq:ent-edge-concentration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-edge-concentration">[eq:ent-edge-concentration]</a> and <a href="#eq:ent-binning" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-binning">[eq:ent-binning]</a> into <a href="#eq:ent-quantized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-quantized">[eq:ent-quantized]</a>. The mesh terms cancel, since $(d+1)\ell-(d/2)(2\ell)=\ell$. This proves <a href="#eq:ent-star-edge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-star-edge">[eq:ent-star-edge]</a>.

Finally the Gaussian determinant formula gives $$\frac1n\mathcal H(Y_{\rm global})
 =\frac{\ell}{2}\log\frac{2\pi{\rm e}\eta}{\pi\rho}
       -\frac{\ell}{n}\sum_i\log|\lambda_i-E-i\eta|.$$ The test function in this sum has Lipschitz constant $O(\eta^{-1})$. Rigidity therefore gives polynomial error in replacing the eigenvalues by Kesten–McKay quantiles. The quantile sum differs from its integral by $O((n\eta)^{-1})$, by summing the lengths of the quantile intervals. An omitted or extra bounded eigenvalue costs $O(\log n/n)$. Lemma <a href="#lem:tree-identities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tree-identities">5</a> gives the remaining regularization error $O(\eta)$. This proves <a href="#eq:ent-global-entropy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ent-global-entropy">[eq:ent-global-entropy]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Removing the approximate eigenvector constraint

The entropy inequality contains one nearly deterministic direction: the sum of the neighbor values is nearly $E$ times the center. We remove that direction so that the limiting Gaussian has identity covariance. Write a star as $(Z,X)$, with $Z\in\mathbb R^\ell$ and $X\in\mathbb R^d\otimes\mathbb R^\ell$, and let $P$ be orthogonal projection of $\mathbb R^d$ onto ${\bf1}^{\perp}$. Define $$\mathcal B(Z,X)=(Z,PX/\sqrt a),\qquad
 R_{\rm res}(Z,X)=\sum_{i=1}^dX_i-EZ,\qquad
 D=(\mathbb R\oplus{\bf1}^{\perp})\otimes\mathbb R^\ell.$$ Here and below projections on spatial coordinates are tensored with the color identity. Let $w$ denote the unit central direction in $\mathbb R\oplus{\bf1}^{\perp}$, and set $$v_i=pw+\sqrt a\,Pe_i,\qquad
 U_i=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(w,v_i)\otimes\mathbb R^\ell .$$ Then $\|v_i\|=1$ and $\langle w,v_i\rangle=p$. Let $P_i$ be orthogonal projection onto $U_i$, put $B_V=\mathcal B(V_{\rm star})$, and let $G$ be an independent standard Gaussian in $D$. Entropies on these spaces use their Euclidean volume.

<a id="stmt-ent-projected-entropy"></a>
<div id="ent:projected-entropy" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:ent-projected-entropy"></a>
**Lemma 9** (Projected-star entropy). *On the graph events above, uniformly for $0\le\epsilon\le1$ and each $i$, $$\label{eq:ent-projected-entropy}
 \mathcal H(\epsilon B_V+G)
       -\frac d2\mathcal H(P_i(\epsilon B_V+G))
 \ge -O(n^{-c}).$$ Moreover $B_V$ has mean zero and second-moment matrix $I_D+O(n^{-1+o(1)})$. Every fixed moment of $P_iB_V$ is invariant up to $O(n^{-1+o(1)})$ under the orthogonal map interchanging $w$ and $v_i$, acting trivially on the colors.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ent-projected-entropy"><summary>Proof of stmt-ent-projected-entropy</summary>

*Proof.* The absolute Jacobian determinant of $(Z,X)\mapsto(\mathcal B(Z,X),R_{\rm res}(Z,X))$ is $(\sqrt d\,a^{-(d-1)/2})^\ell$. For each color, the residual variance of $Y$, averaged over centers, is at most $\eta/(\pi\rho)$, since $$(A-E)\Gamma(A-E)
 =\frac{\eta}{\pi\rho}
   \frac{(A-E)^2}{(A-E)^2+\eta^2}
 \le\frac{\eta}{\pi\rho}I.$$ The eigenvector equations give a residual $O(n^{-1+o(1)})$ for $V$, uniformly over locations and choices: $|\lambda_{i(j)}-E|\le S^2/n$. More precisely, put $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\lambda_{i(j)}-E:1\le j\le\ell)$. At a uniform center $v$, the deterministic residual is $\epsilon\Delta V(v)$, whose second-moment matrix is $\epsilon^2\Delta^2$ by eigenvector orthogonality. The Gaussian colors are independent and centered conditionally on the location, so cross terms vanish and <a id="eq:ent-residual-covariance"></a>$$\label{eq:ent-residual-covariance}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}\bigl(R_{\rm res}(F_{\rm star})\bigr)
 \le \frac{\eta}{\pi\rho}I_\ell+\epsilon^2\Delta^2
 \le \left(\frac{\eta}{\pi\rho}+\frac{S^4}{n^2}\right)I_\ell.$$ The relative correction inside the logarithmic entropy bound satisfies $$\log\left(1+\frac{\pi\rho S^4}{n^2\eta}\right)
 =O\bigl(n^{-2+\alpha+o(1)}\bigr).$$ The maximum-entropy bound by covariance and the fact that conditioning lowers entropy thus give $$\mathcal H(R_{\rm res}(F_{\rm star})\mid
                    \mathcal B(F_{\rm star}))
 \le \frac{\ell}{2}\log\frac{2\pi{\rm e}\eta}{\pi\rho}
          +O(n^{-c}).$$

By the typical-star estimate at $E+i\eta$ and the tree covariance in Lemma <a href="#lem:tree-identities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tree-identities">5</a>, the covariance of the noise $\mathcal B(Y_{\rm star})$ differs from $I_D$ by a polynomially small error outside a polynomially small fraction of centers. Gaussian total variation after translation, followed by the entropy-continuity argument in Lemma <a href="#ent:star-edge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ent:star-edge">8</a>, allows this noise to be replaced by $G$ at polynomial entropy cost.
